原题选用与知识能力

②8个地点形容词：ancient古老、cheap便宜、crowded拥挤、expensive昂贵、modern现代、popular受欢迎、rural乡村的、urban城市的。		用live、walk、not call、stay、try完成5个过去经历句，并按原题与同伴核对。另做原题：写3句假期经历，两真一假。
做8个地点形容词与8张照片的匹配。三道原题各自保留原要求；猜真假与猜形容词的另两道题不做。		Ex.1 单列动词过去式及轮流朗读

You'll see marchers in traditional costumes and people waving flags. They celebrate people from around the world living together in peace. They show the culture and traditions of many countries. Lots of groups from the community are also in the parade. If you like boats, you can watch the boat parade on the water. The boats are colourful, with Emirati decorations. Come to the National Day Super Sale for both cheap shopping (with very low prices) and fireworks -on the main day, the sky will be lit up with an incredible show of noise and colour. (also) 5 I do not accept advertising cookies. I delete cookies from my smartphone. (too) Compound sentences C, :::u A compound sentence contains two independent clauses (clauses which have )>s: their own subject and verb). Use a conjunction, such as and or but, to link two s: independent clauses. )> Use and to add information. :::u Lina doesn't have a social media account. She doesn't want one. -+ Lina doesn't have a social media account, and she doesn't want one. Use but to give contrasting or different information or ideas. Martin reads books on a tablet. Jose likes to read printed books. -+ Martin reads books on a tablet, but Jose likes to read printed books. Use a comma before and or but in a compound sentence.

FUTURE FORMS Decisions and plans Gl ;::u Use will to talk about decisions made at the time you are speaking. ► s: Use will+ not (won't) for the negative form. s: From now on, I will trl.f to recl.fcle more. I won't for9et. ► ;::u Use be going to+ infinitive to talk about decisions made before you speak or a general plan that can change in the future. Biju's 9oin9 to present information about the forests next week. Use the present continuous (be + verb + -ing) to talk about arrangements made for the future that are difficult to change. An arrangement is a fixed plan, often with another person and / or at a planned time. Next week, manl.f people are comin9 to the meetin9 on 9lobal warmin9. Predictions When the prediction is based on personal opinion, use will or won't to say you are certain about a future event. Use will probably or probably won't when you are a bit less certain. Scientists think summers with verl.f hi9h temperatures will happen more of ten from now on. The number of polar bears will probabl 90 down b 30% b 2050. l.f i./ i./ Because of 9lobal warmin9, we won't have as manl.f maple trees in the future. So we probabl won't have l.f much maple S1.f rup. Use be going to+ infinitive to talk about a future event when there is evidence that it is likely to happen. Sea ice is meltin9 because of 9lobal warmin9. Soon there isn't 9oin9 to be anl.f sea ice in the Arctic in the summer. You can often use either will or be going to for predictions with a very similar meaning. Scientists have found that sea levels will I are 9oin9 to rise over the next 50 I.fears.

It gives you a place to r­ r­ write reasons for a point (you agree with an idea) or against a point (you disagree with V\ it). It also helps you organize support for your reasons (evidence for why your opinion is correct). Using a persuasion map can help you organize your ideas when you want to get others to agree with your point of view.

Book 2 · U6 · LSLanguage development讨论个人幸福感原因不选做本题不选；不把其中的写句、问答、讨论或核对动作转移到其他题。

Work with a partner and answer the questions. 1 Which of the reasons for happiness at work from the chart are the same as yours? Which reasons in the chart are the most important for you? Why? 2 What other reasons for happiness at work do you have? 3 PHRASAL VERBS Phrasal verbs are verbs with one or more particles (prepositions or adverbs). The verb � and the particle(s) have a specific meaning when you use them together. This meaning n can be very different from the meaning of the same verb on its own. Common ► m particles in phrasal verbs include up, down, back, out, on, off, in, by, etc. Use a c dictionary to check meanings of phrasal verbs. ► Verb: I ran out of the room. (= left the room running) � Phrasal verb: I ran out of time. (= used all of something so there is none left) Look at the phrasal verbs and their definitions below. find out to get information about something, or to learn a fact for the first time get by to be able to live or deal with a situation with difficulty, usually by having just enough of something you need, such as money look at to think about a subject carefully so that you can make a decision about it run out to use all of something so that there is none left take over to start doing something that another person was doing before write down to write something on a piece of paper so that you do not forget it In some phrasal verbs, such as write down, it is possible to put other words between the verb and the particle. Use a dictionary to check which phrasal verbs can have words between the verb and the particle. I wrote down all the details. I wrote all the details down.

THE SECOND CONDITIONAL Use the second conditional to describe imagined situations that would happen Gl :::u under certain conditions in the present or future. ► s: Use the past simple in the if-clause to describe the imagined situation. Use a modal s: verb (e.g. would I could) in the main clause to describe the predicted result or possible ► outcome. The contraction of would is 'd (I'd, you'd, we'd, they'd, he'd, she'd, it'd). :::u The if-clause can come before or after the main clause. Use a comma between the clauses when the if-clause comes first. if-clause main clause If the� took all the space junk awa�, there wouldn't be a problem an� more. main clause if-clause There's not much chance an� space junk would hit �ou if �ou were outside on a normal da�. Notice that in the if-clause, the past form were (be) is used for all persons (I, you, we, they, he, she, it). If a ver� fast spacecraft were invented, people could travel to see other stars.

PRESENT PERFECT et:: The present perfect connects something that happened in the past with a � present result. It's well known that thin9s such as electricit�. the telephone and television have chan9ed our lives si9nircantl�. � (= these things were invented in the past and still affect our lives in the present) � CJ Use present perfect if you don't know exactly when something happened, or it is not important to say exactly when it happened: In �our book, �ou discuss how technolo9� has chan9ed the wa� we celebrate important holida�s. (= the change(s) happened in the past, it's not important or certain when, and affect our celebrations in the present) Or when something happened frequently, or over a period of time: I have noticed that there are often more similarities than differences between people. (= I noticed this several times in the past and it affects my opinion in the present). Compare this to the past simple, which describes finished actions that don't affect the present: As a child, I lived in Japan, Spain and E9�pt. M� parents were En91ish teachers, so we travelled a lot. To form the present perfect, we use have/has+ past participle. In questions, use have/has+ subject+ past participle. The past participle can be regular or irregular, e.g. change (present) I changed (past simple) I changed (regular past participle) choose (present) I chose (past simple) I chosen (irregular past participle). Statement forms - positive and negative The television has chan9ed Mahmood and Nila have not understood what the� need to do next. Question forms (Wh- questions and Yes/No questions) What have decided to wear to the part�? Has Caroline invited �ou to her part�?

My purpose statement: __________________ Evaluating interview questions V\ In order to get interesting and relevant information from an interview, you need to r­ r­ ask good questions. V\ Good interview questions have these characteristics: 1 They are open. Open questions allow many possible answers. Closed questions limit the possible answers. Closed questions: Do �ou prefer the fashions of a decade a90 or of toda�? (Answer choices: a decade ago I today) Are �ou interested in fashion? (Answer choices: yes I no) Open questions: What decade do �ou think had the most interestin9 fashions? How did �ou become interested in fashion? 2 They are not biased. Biased questions encourage the interviewee to answer in a certain way. Biased question: Don't �OU think fashions have become less attractive in the last rve �ears? (Encourages a yes answer) Unbiased question: What's �our opinion of how fashion has chan9ed in the last rve �ears? (Doesn't encourage a particular answer) 3 They only have one part. Two-part questions can confuse the interviewee. After they answer the first part, they might forget what the second part was. Two-part question: What is �our opinion of how fashions have chan9ed in the past rve �ears and what do �ou think of toda�'s fashions? One-part question: What do �ou think of toda�'s fashions? 4 They are focused. Focused questions make it clear what type of answer you're looking for. Unfocused question: What do �ou think about the future? (It's not clear what aspect of the future you want to know about.) Focused question: What do �ou think fashions will be like in the future? 5 They relate to your purpose. Purpose: The purpose of the interview is to explore the future of fashion. Unrelated question: Who is �our favourite fashion desi9ner? (This doesn't relate to the future of fashion.) Related question: How do �ou think fashion will chan9e in the next ten �ears?
